### [66] [Exact Semiclassical Phase Shifts for Relativistic Atom Interferometers in Flat Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10042)
*Hunter Swan,Jason M. Hogan*

Main category: physics.atom-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives relativistically exact analytical expressions for atom interferometer phase shifts in flat spacetime for various pulse sequences (Mach-Zehnder, resonant, large momentum transfer), including both clock (single-photon) and Raman/Bragg (two-photon) interferometers. The leading-order phase shift $ω_a g T^2/c$ is corrected to $ω_a (1 + ω_a/(2m))(e^{-gT/c}-1)^2 c/g$ when full relativistic kinematics are considered.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Atom interferometry is a sensitive tool for measuring relativistic effects, but no non-trivial exact relativistic solutions for atom interferometer phase shifts existed prior to this work.

Method: The authors derive relativistically exact expressions within the semiclassical approximation for atom interferometer pulse sequences in flat spacetime, including full relativistic kinematics for both single-photon (clock) and two-photon (Raman/Bragg) interferometers.

Result: The relativistic correction transforms the leading-order Mach-Zehnder clock atom interferometer phase shift from $ω_a g T^2/c$ to $ω_a (1 + ω_a/(2m))(e^{-gT/c}-1)^2 c/g$. Exact phase shifts are provided for multiple interferometer geometries.

Conclusion: Relativistically exact phase shift expressions are now available for experimentally relevant atom interferometer configurations, enabling more precise modeling of relativistic effects in these systems.

Abstract: Atom interferometry is a sensitive tool for measuring relativistic effects, but there are no known non-trivial exact solutions for relativistic atom interferometer phase shifts. Here we derive relativistically exact expressions within the usual semiclassical approximation for a wide range of experimentally interesting atom interferometer pulse sequences in flat spacetime, including Mach-Zehnder, resonant, and large momentum transfer interferometer geometries. As an example, the leading order phase shift $ω_a g T^2/c$ for a Mach-Zehnder clock atom interferometer is found to become $ω_a \left(1 + \frac{ω_a}{2m}\right)(e^{-gT/c}-1)^2 c/g$ when all relativistic kinematics are included. We calculate exact phase shifts for both clock (single-photon) interferometers and Raman or Bragg (two-photon) interferometers.

</details>


### [67] [On the measurability of Wigner time delays at shape resonances in photodetachment of polyatomic anions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10397)
*Jan Dvořák,Jakub Benda,Thomas N. Rescigno,Cynthia S. Trevisan,Robert R. Lucchese,C. William McCurdy*

Main category: physics.atom-ph

TL;DR: This paper predicts attosecond photodetachment time delays in negative ions (NO₃⁻) using RABBIT and streaking techniques, showing that long-range Coulomb interactions are absent in anions, enabling direct access to Wigner time delays (up to ~1 fs) for ejection directions close to light polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The long-range Coulomb interaction in photoionization experiments (e.g., streaking, RABBIT) obscures low-energy Wigner time delays. Photodetachment from negative ions eliminates this Coulomb tail, potentially revealing clean, large time delays (up to 1 fs) at low energies, but no such experiments have been performed on polyatomic anions.

Method: The authors simulate RABBIT and streaking experiments for valence and core electron photodetachment of NO₃⁻. They analyze the angular dependence of measured delays and compare them to Wigner delays, accounting for multi-partial-wave contributions in polyatomic anions.

Result: Measured delays from RABBIT/streaking generally differ from Wigner delays due to angular dependence from multiple partial waves. However, when ejection is near the polarization direction, the measured delays directly correspond to Wigner delays. Core photodetachment with X-rays is proposed as a promising experiment.

Conclusion: Photodetachment from polyatomic anions can directly reveal Wigner time delays (up to ~1 fs) at low energies, provided measurements are made along the polarization axis. This offers a clean probe of electron-molecule interactions without Coulomb distortion.

Abstract: The energy dependence of the complex phases of electron continuum wave functions carries information about electron dynamics. Streaking and attosecond interference experiments (called RABBIT) seek to measure this energy dependence, and therefore, the time delays of ionization. The long-range Coulomb interaction dominates in those experiments, and can obscure the low-energy features of the Wigner time delays that are the object of the measurement. Photodetachment of electrons from negative ions has no long-range Coulomb interaction, and RABBIT and streaking measurements of photodetachment delays have the potential to reveal time delays of up to one femtosecond in low-energy features. We predict the results of such experiments on a particularly interesting polyatomic example, the nitrate anion (NO$_3^-$), for both valence and core electron detachment. We simulate the experiments in these cases and analyze the underlying physics of measurements on polyatomic anions where many electron partial waves contribute and find that the angular dependence of the measured delays generally differs from the Wigner delays. However, we demonstrate that measurements performed for ejection directions close to the polarization of the light sources can directly access the Wigner delays that give a time-dependent window on electron-molecule interactions. A promising experiment involving core photodetachment of NO$_3^-$ with X-rays is proposed.

</details>

### [68] [Are We Ready for AI-Driven Discovery? AI Verification Before the Next Fundamental Physics Breakthrough](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10039)
*Gaia Grosso,Vinicius Mikuni,Lukas Heinrich*

Main category: physics.data-an

TL;DR: The VERaiPHY initiative provides frameworks for rigorous ML assessment in fundamental physics, emphasizing verification, inductive bias limitations, sample complexity bounds, and the evolving role of physicists as evaluators for reliable AI-driven discovery.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: As ML systems become increasingly autonomous in physics discovery workflows, ensuring their reliability for scientific claims is critical, motivating the need for systematic validation frameworks.

Method: Synthesis of the VERaiPHY (Validation & Evaluation for Robust AI in PHYsics) initiative's frameworks for assessing ML across particle physics, astrophysics, and cosmology, contextualizing ML within the statistical discovery workflow.

Result: Established when verification is essential; identified fundamental limitations including unavoidable inductive bias, sample complexity bounds on learning, and experimental constraints limiting discovery.

Conclusion: Responsible integration of ML in physics requires understanding both its transformative potential and intrinsic boundaries, with physicists serving as experimental designers and evaluators whose judgments encode scientific rigor into AI systems.

Abstract: Machine learning (ML) has become integral to fundamental physics, accelerating statistical workflows from data acquisition through inference and hypothesis testing. As ML systems grow increasingly autonomous, ensuring their reliability for discovery claims becomes critical. This review synthesizes the VERaiPHY (Validation & Evaluation for Robust AI in PHYsics) initiative's frameworks for rigorous ML assessment across particle physics, astrophysics, and cosmology. We establish when verification is essential by contextualizing ML within the statistical discovery workflow. We emphasize fundamental limitations: inductive bias is unavoidable, sample complexity bounds learning, and experimental constraints limit discovery. We reflect on physicists' evolving role as both experimental designers and evaluators whose judgments encode scientific rigor into AI systems. Responsible integration requires understanding ML's transformative potential alongside its intrinsic boundaries.

</details>


### [69] [Quantification of Electron Energy-Loss Spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10693)
*Pavel Potapov,Giulio Guzzinati*

Main category: physics.data-an

TL;DR: Summarizes recent developments in EELS quantification flow implemented in CEOS Panta Rhei and TEMDM software, serving as a technical reference for the algorithms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document and provide a technical reference for the algorithms used in EELS quantification within the CEOS Panta Rhei and TEMDM software.

Method: Summarization of recent developments in EELS quantification flow as implemented in software.

Result: A technical reference document describing the EELS quantification algorithms and their implementation.

Conclusion: The manuscript serves as a technical reference for EELS quantification algorithms in CEOS Panta Rhei and TEMDM software.

Abstract: This manuscript summarizes the recent developments in EELS quantification flow as will be implemented in the CEOS Panta Rhei and TEMDM software. This should serve as a technical reference for the algorithms used in the software.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.supr-con'></div>

### [70] [Landau-Zener tunneling and quantum interference of Andreev states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09859)
*Mikhail S. Kalenkov,Andrei D. Zaikin*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper derives an effective Hamiltonian for Andreev states in non-equilibrium superconducting nanojunctions, calculates current under voltage bias, and predicts coherent supercurrent oscillations due to quantum interference of Andreev states in junctions with near-unity transmission.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum dynamics of Andreev states in superconducting nanojunctions out of equilibrium and derive an effective theoretical framework for describing them.

Method: Microscopic theory derivation of an effective Hamiltonian for Andreev states; solving a Schrödinger-like equation for Andreev level wave functions; evaluating electric current under external voltage bias.

Result: Quantum interference of Andreev states can produce pronounced coherent oscillations of the supercurrent as a function of the Josephson phase in junctions with barrier transmissions slightly below unity.

Conclusion: The effect has implications for junctions with diffusive barriers, suggesting broader applicability beyond ballistic junctions.

Abstract: With the aid of a microscopic theory we derive an effective Hamiltonian that controls quantum dynamics of Andreev states in superconducting nanojunctions out of equilibrium. Resolving the corresponding Schrödinger-like equation we obtain the "wave functions" for Andreev levels and evaluate electric current across the junction in the presence of an external voltage bias. We demonstrate that quantum interference of Andreev states may yield pronounced coherent oscillations of the supercurrent as a function of the Josephson phase in junctions with barrier transmissions slightly below unity. Further implications of this novel effect are expected for junctions with diffusive barriers.

</details>


### [71] [Superconducting dome and field-enhanced superconductivity of PLD synthesized Nd1-xEuxNiO2 thin films](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10332)
*Wenlong Yang,Qiang Zhao,Xingke Fu,Gaofei Ren,Zhongjing Wu,Zhen Chen,Jianping Sun,Boseng Wang,Jiacai Nie,Pengtao Yang,Jinguang Cheng*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This study demonstrates that PLD-fabricated Nd1-xEuxNiO2 thin films exhibit a superconducting dome (0.2<x<0.5) with optimal Tc~31K at x=0.3, wider doping range and higher Tc than MBE-grown films, along with robust field-enhanced/re-entrant superconductivity and nonlinear Hall resistance, highlighting the role of magnetic Eu2+ ions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether PLD can produce high-quality infinite-layer nickelate superconducting films with superior properties compared to molecular beam epitaxy (MBE) and chemical synthesis methods, and to investigate the role of magnetic rare-earth Eu2+ ions in the exotic superconducting behavior.

Method: PLD was used to grow Nd1-xEuxNiO2 thin films (0<x<0.7) followed by topotactic reduction with CaH2; resistivity, magneto-transport, and Hall resistance measurements were conducted to characterize superconducting properties.

Result: A superconducting dome exists for 0.2<x<0.5; optimal Tc~31K at x=0.3 (higher than other vacuum techniques); robust field-enhanced and re-entrant superconductivity observed in both underdoped and overdoped regimes; nonlinear Hall resistance at low temperatures above onset Tc suggests magnetic impurity scattering.

Conclusion: PLD is an ideal method for fabricating high-quality, high-Tc Nd1-xEuxNiO2 superconducting films, and magnetic Eu2+ ions play a crucial role in the exotic physical properties of infinite-layer nickelates.

Abstract: We report on the synthesis of infinite-layer Nd1-xEuxNiO2 (0<x<0.7) thin films using pulsed laser deposition (PLD) followed by topotactic reduction with CaH2. Resistivity measurements on these films reveal a superconducting dome within the doping range 0.2<x<0.5, which is wider than that achieved by molecular beam epitaxy and comparable to that obtained by chemical synthesis. The x=0.3 PLD film exhibits the optimal superconducting transition temperature Tc~31 K, much higher than those grown by other vacuum epitaxial techniques. This result indicates that PLD is an ideal approach for fabricating high-quality, high-Tc Nd1-xEuxNiO2 superconducting films. Magneto-transport measurements reveal robust field-enhanced and re-entrant superconductivity in both underdoped and overdoped regimes. At low temperatures just above the onset Tc, the Hall resistance exhibits nonlinear behavior, which may originate from magnetic impurity scattering. These results highlight the crucial role of magnetic rare-earth Eu2+ ions in producing the exotic physical properties of the infinite-layer nickelates.

</details>


### [72] [Anomalous field evolution of the mixed-state linewidth in the second superconducting dome of LaFeAsO$_{1-x}M_x$ ($M={\rm F,H}$)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10348)
*Rustem Khasanov,Pierre Dalmas de Réotier,Samuele Sanna,Gianrico Lamura,Hubertus Luetkens,Matteo Moroni,Pietro Carretta,Rhea Kappenberger,Rowena Wachtel,Bernd Büchner,Sabine Wurmehl,Nikolai D. Zhigadlo*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This μSR study compares the internal-field distribution in the mixed state of LaFeAsO-based superconductors from the first (SC1, F-doped) and second (SC2, H-doped) superconducting domes. While SC1 shows conventional monotonic decrease of superconducting linewidth with field, SC2 exhibits an anomalous local maximum near 3T, attributed to enhanced Pauli-paramagnetic effects, revealing distinct electronic characters of the two domes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate and compare the vortex state properties and internal-field distributions in the two distinct superconducting domes (SC1 and SC2) of the LaFeAsO₁₋ₓMₓ (M=F,H) family, aiming to understand their different electronic characters.

Method: Transverse-field muon-spin rotation/relaxation (μSR) measurements on LaFeAsO₀.₈₉F₀.₁₁ (SC1) and LaFeAsO₀.₇₅H₀.₂₅ (SC2) samples, with analysis of linewidth (σₛ꜀) as functions of field and temperature after subtracting normal-state Knight shift broadening.

Result: Below T_c, both samples show vortex lattice formation. At 4K, SC1 exhibits monotonic decrease of σₛ꜀ with increasing field, while SC2 shows a pronounced local maximum near 3T. A contour plot of σₛ꜀(T,H) for SC2 reveals a ridge of local maxima shifting to lower fields upon warming, disappearing near T_c.

Conclusion: The anomalous field evolution in SC2 indicates an additional field-induced contribution from enhanced Pauli-paramagnetic effects, highlighting distinct electronic character between the two superconducting domes.

Abstract: We report a transverse-field muon-spin rotation/relaxation ($μ$SR) study of the internal-field distribution in the mixed state of LaFeAsO$_{0.89}$F$_{0.11}$ and LaFeAsO$_{0.75}$H$_{0.25}$, representative of the first (SC1) and second (SC2) superconducting domes of the LaFeAsO$_{1-x}M_x$ ($M={\rm F,H}$) family, respectively. Below the superconducting transition temperature $T_{\rm c}$, the linewidth of the internal-field distribution increases in both samples, indicating the formation of a vortex lattice. Above $T_{\rm c}$, the linewidth remains field dependent and increases approximately linearly with field, consistent with broadening of the powder spectrum caused by an anisotropic Knight shift. After subtraction of this normal-state contribution, the superconducting linewidth $σ_{\rm sc}$ exhibits qualitatively different field dependences in the two samples. At 4K, the SC1 ($x_{\rm F}=0.11$) sample shows the expected monotonic decrease with increasing field, whereas the SC2 ($x_{\rm H}=0.25$) sample develops a pronounced local maximum near 3T. A contour representation of $σ_{\rm sc}(T,H)$ further reveals a ridge of local maxima whose field position, $H_{σ,\max}(T)$, shifts to lower fields upon warming and disappears near $T_{\rm c}$. The anomalous field evolution observed in the SC2 sample is consistent with an additional field-induced contribution associated with enhanced Pauli-paramagnetic effects, highlighting the distinct electronic character of the two superconducting domes.

</details>


### [73] [Effect of superconducting fluctuations on nonreciprocal dichroism and gyrotropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10464)
*Alex Levchenko*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper theoretically demonstrates that nonreciprocal, odd-in-wavevector gyrotropic conductivity arises from fluctuating Cooper pairs above Tc in 2D noncentrosymmetric superconductors, producing directional dichroism and gyrotropic birefringence that are critically enhanced near Tc, dominated by particle-hole asymmetry, and connected to the superconducting diode effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how fluctuation-induced Cooper pairs above Tc contribute to nonreciprocal optical responses (gyrotropic conductivity) in noncentrosymmetric superconductors, and to connect these effects to related phenomena like the superconducting diode effect and magnetochiral anisotropy.

Method: Time-dependent Ginzburg-Landau theory generalized to include particle-hole asymmetry and cubic Lifshitz invariant; Aslamazov-Larkin contribution to gyrotropic conductivity computed in closed form with full frequency dependence; Kramers-Kronig analysis for dual components.

Result: Dissipative part (directional dichroism) is odd in frequency, nonmonotonic, peaking near the Cooper-pair decay rate. Reactive part (gyrotropic birefringence) remains finite in static limit and diverges as T→Tc. Both require broken inversion and time-reversal symmetry, depend on particle-hole asymmetry, and are critically enhanced near Tc, dominating the normal-state baseline.

Conclusion: Fluctuation-induced gyrotropy provides a sensitive probe of superconducting dynamics near Tc, with implications for NV-center quantum noise spectroscopy in materials like gated MoS₂.

Abstract: We study the spatially dispersive conductivity of a two-dimensional noncentrosymmetric superconductor, demonstrating that it acquires a nonreciprocal, odd-in-wavevector component from fluctuation-induced Cooper pairs above the critical temperature $T_c$. Utilizing time-dependent Ginzburg-Landau theory generalized to include particle-hole asymmetry and the cubic Lifshitz invariant of trigonal superconductors, we compute the Aslamazov-Larkin contribution to the gyrotropic conductivity in closed form, including its complete frequency dependence. The dissipative part describes nonreciprocal directional dichroism: it is odd in frequency and displays a nonmonotonic dependence, peaking at frequencies comparable to the decay rate of fluctuating Cooper pairs. Its Kramers-Kronig dual component describes gyrotropic birefringence, which remains finite in the static limit and is strongly enhanced as the temperature approaches $T_c$. Both effects require simultaneously broken inversion and time-reversal symmetries, are dependent on particle-hole asymmetry in close analogy to the fluctuation Hall effect, and trace to the same asymmetric Cooper-pair dispersion responsible for the superconducting diode effect and the giant magnetochiral anisotropy observed near $T_c$. This critical enhancement dominates over the smooth normal-state gyrotropy, which we evaluate for the same band model as a baseline. Finally, we frame our analysis within the context of gated transition metal dichalcogenides like MoS$_2$, discussing the implications for probing superconducting dynamics through nitrogen-vacancy-center quantum noise spectroscopy.

</details>


### [74] [Soft point-contact Andreev reflection spectroscopy in a palm-type cubic anvil-pressure cell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10668)
*Qingxin Dong,Fengrui Shi,Yan Zhang,Tong Shi,Yi Liu,Shaoheng Ruan,Zhongjin Wu,Jianping Sun,Zhaoming Tian,Yoshiya Uwatoko,Guanghan Cao,Xin Lu,Bosen Wang,Jin-Guang Cheng*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper presents a newly developed soft point-contact Andreev reflection spectroscopy (PCARS) technique integrated with a palm-type cubic anvil pressure cell, enabling stable point-contact junctions under hydrostatic pressures up to 15 GPa. The method is validated on Nb and applied to study the unconventional superconductivity in CsCr3Sb5 and La2PrNi2O7, revealing zero-bias conductance peaks and providing spectroscopic evidence for unconventional pairing symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust experimental platform that bridges macroscopic electrical transport and microscopic spectroscopic probes, enabling investigation of pairing symmetry in pressure-induced unconventional superconductors.

Method: Combined a substrate anchoring strategy with an external wire-splitting technique to implement soft point-contact Andreev reflection spectroscopy (PCARS) inside a palm-type cubic anvil pressure cell, achieving stable multiple point-contact junctions under hydrostatic pressures up to 15 GPa.

Result: Benchmark on Nb showed high reproducibility with a zero-temperature superconducting gap ratio of 3.3. Application to CsCr3Sb5 and La2PrNi2O7 revealed pronounced zero-bias conductance peaks, and their evolution with temperature, magnetic field, and pressure was characterized.

Conclusion: The work establishes a robust experimental platform that bridges macroscopic transport and microscopic spectroscopy, opening new avenues for investigating pairing symmetry in a wide range of pressure-induced unconventional superconductors.

Abstract: We have implemented soft point-contact Andreev reflection spectroscopy (PCARS) in a palm-type cubic anvil pressure cell by combining a substrate anchoring strategy with an external wire-splitting technique. This design enables the stable formation of multiple point contact junctions under hydrostatic pressures up to 15 GPa. Benchmark measurements on the elemental superconductor Nb demonstrate high reproducibility and yield a zero-temperature superconducting gap with a gap ratio of 3.3. We further apply this technique to the Kagome metal superconductor CsCr3Sb5 and the bilayer nickelate superconductor La2PrNi2O7. Pronounced zero-bias conductance peaks are observed, and their evolution with temperature, magnetic field and applied pressure is investigated, together with the superconducting gap magnitude and possible pairing symmetries. These measurements provide spectroscopic evidence consistent with unconventional superconductivity in these materials. Our work establishes a robust experimental platform that bridges macroscopic electrical transport and microscopic spectroscopic probes, opening a new avenue for investigating pairing symmetry in a wide range of pressure-induced unconventional superconductors.

</details>


### [75] [Evolution from an acoustic-plasmon-mediated superconductivity to an acoustic-phonon-mediated superconductivity in bilayers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10835)
*Shuyang Wang,Sankar Das Sarma,Jay D. Sau*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper revisits acoustic plasmon-mediated superconductivity in bilayer systems (light layer + heavy layer) using Eliashberg theory, studying the evolution from plasmon-mediated to phonon-mediated pairing as the heavy layer mass varies, including nonadiabatic effects via a frequency cutoff.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by recent developments in van der Waals heterostructures, the authors revisit the acoustic plasmon mechanism of superconductivity in bilayer systems composed of a light layer and a heavy layer.

Method: Eliashberg theory is employed to model the retarded attractive interaction between electrons in the light layer via exchange of virtual plasmons in the heavy layer, using screened interaction within the random phase approximation. Nonadiabatic effects for arbitrary mass ratios are studied using a frequency cutoff in Eliashberg theory.

Result: The paper explores the evolution of $T_c$ as the heavy layer mass increases by orders of magnitude relative to the light layer electronic mass, transitioning from acoustic plasmon-mediated superconductivity (low HL mass) to the Born-Oppenheimer regime of acoustic phonon-mediated retarded pairing (high HL mass).

Conclusion: The heavy HL mass limit obeys Migdal's theorem due to the small mass ratio. The study provides $T_c$ as a function of the frequency cutoff, systematically addressing nonadiabatic effects for arbitrary mass ratios where no small parameter exists.

Abstract: Motivated by recent developments in van der Waals heterostructures, we revisit the acoustic plasmon mechanism of superconductivity in bilayer systems composed of a light layer (LL) and heavy layer (HL) by employing Eliashberg theory. The exchange of virtual plasmons in the HL can lead to a retarded in time attractive interaction between electrons of LL that we model through the screened interaction in the bilayer system within the random phase approximation. We explore the evolution from acoustic plasmon mediated superconductivity to phonon mediated superconductivity by studying the evolution of $T_c$ as the HL mass is increased by a few orders of magnitude compared with the electronic mass in LL. The lower HL mass corresponds to the bilayer acoustic plasmon, while the latter regime is closer to the Born-Oppenheimer regime of acoustic phonon mediated strongly retarded pairing. The heavy HL mass limit is known to obey Migdal's theorem by virtue of the small ratio of the two individual layer masses. We study the nonadiabatic effects for the arbitrary mass ratio with no small parameter systematically by using a frequency cut off in the Eliashberg theory, providing $T_c$ as a function of this cut off.

</details>


### [76] [A minimal model for the Weyl nodes and Fermi arcs of PtBi$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10937)
*Tobias Cristófoli,Manuel Alonso Lemos,Jorge I. Facio,Pablo S. Cornaglia*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The authors develop a minimal, symmetry-constrained low-energy model for trigonal PtBi₂ guided by DFT, capturing the evolution of Weyl nodes, spin-momentum locking of Fermi arcs, and providing a foundation for studying surface superconductivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing theoretical descriptions of surface superconductivity in PtBi₂ rely on effective models not directly tied to the material's microscopic electronic structure; a model rooted in ab initio calculations is needed.

Method: A minimal low-energy model is constructed guided by density functional theory (DFT) calculations, fully constrained by crystalline and time-reversal symmetries.

Result: The model captures the evolution of Weyl nodes with spin-orbit coupling (including node annihilations), reproduces the spin-momentum locking of surface Fermi arcs, and correctly describes the orbital content and band topology of PtBi₂.

Conclusion: This microscopically grounded model provides a reliable starting point for further studies of the topological and superconducting properties of PtBi₂.

Abstract: Weyl semimetals host topologically protected Fermi arcs on their surfaces, originating from the Chern number of the bulk Weyl nodes. In trigonal PtBi$_2$, superconducting signatures have been associated with the Fermi arcs. Theoretical descriptions of this surface superconductivity have so far relied on effective models that are not directly tied to the microscopic electronic structure of the material. In this work we develop a minimal description of the low-energy bands guided by density functional theory calculations and fully constrained by the relevant crystalline and time-reversal symmetries. The model captures the evolution of the Weyl nodes with spin-orbit coupling, including node annihilations, and is able to describe the spin-momentum locking of the surface Fermi arcs. It reproduces the orbital content and the band topology of PtBi$_2$ and provides a starting point for further studies.

</details>


### [77] [Ferroelectric--Superconducting Interaction in Epitaxial YBa2Cu3O7-δ/BaTiO3 Films](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11003)
*Louis Oppong-Antwi,David L. Cortie,Qi Zhang,Avi Bendavid,Nagarajan Valanoor,Wendy Purches,Golrokh Akhgar*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: The paper reports that proximity to a ferroelectric (BaTiO3) suppresses the superconducting transition temperature of YBa2Cu3O7-δ in heterostructures, with Tc dropping from ~86 K (single-layer YBCO) to ~75 K (resistivity) and ~55 K (magnetization) in YBCO/BTO/YBCO stacks, confirming ferroelectric-superconducting coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Microscopic theories predict strong modification of a superconductor's critical temperature when in proximity to a ferroelectric, but experimental evidence has been lacking. This work aims to provide such evidence using BTO/YBCO heterostructures.

Method: BTO/YBCO heterostructures were grown on SrTiO3(001) by pulsed laser deposition with controlled oxygen stoichiometry. Characterized by piezoresponse force microscopy (ferroelectric switching), neutron reflectometry (interface roughness), and resistivity/magnetization measurements (superconducting Tc).

Result: Single-layer YBCO exhibits Tc ~86 K (resistivity) and ~80-85 K (magnetization). Heterostructure (YBCO/BTO/YBCO) shows reduced Tc ~75 K (resistivity) and ~55 K (magnetization) with lower overall susceptibility. Neutron reflectometry confirms low interface roughness essential for interfacial coupling.

Conclusion: The suppressed Tc in heterostructures provides experimental evidence of ferroelectric-superconducting coupling. Multiple characterization techniques are needed to probe subtle coupling features. BTO/YBCO heterostructures are promising for tunable oxide electronics and reconfigurable superconducting devices.

Abstract: Microscopic theories predict that the critical temperature of a superconducting layer can be strongly modified in proximity to a ferroelectric; however, experimental evidence is lacking. We report on BaTiO3 (BTO)/YBa2Cu3O7-δ (YBCO) heterostructures grown on SrTiO3 (001) substrates using pulsed laser deposition (PLD), with precise control of growth conditions and oxygen stoichiometry. Piezoresponse force microscopy confirms ferroelectric switching within both single-layer and heterostructured films. Neutron reflectometry measurements show low roughness parameters for the buried interfaces, which is critical for mediating the interfacial coupling between superconductivity and ferroelectric polarization. The superconducting transition temperature ($T_{\mathrm{C}}^{R}$) measured from the $R(T)$ curve of the single-layer YBCO was approximately 86 K, and a transition temperature ($T_{\mathrm{C}}^{M}$) of approximately 80-85 K was observed from the $M(T)$ curve. For the heterostructure film (YBCO/BTO/YBCO) grown under the same conditions, both resistivity and magnetization measurements indicate lower transition temperatures of approximately 75 K and approximately 55 K, respectively, as well as a lower overall susceptibility. These findings highlight the need to combine multiple techniques to characterize BTO/YBCO heterostructures and identify subtle features of the ferroelectric--superconducting coupling. This establishes BTO/YBCO heterostructures as promising systems for tunable oxide electronics and reconfigurable superconducting devices.

</details>


### [78] [Strain-Tuned Nodal Superconductivity in the Charge-Ordered Kagome Metal CsV$_3$Sb$_5$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11115)
*Yusuke Takeuchi,Akito Kobayashi,Saki Uchida,Takumi Nagao,Seigo Ogawa,Rui Zhou,Shinji Kawasaki,Fei Song,Hao Ni,Yong Zhao,Guo-qing Zheng*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper uses ¹²¹Sb NQR under uniaxial pressure to study superconductivity in CsV₃Sb₅, finding that tensile strain enhances Tc independently of CDW order. At ε=+0.90%, a double transition appears: an upper nodal gap state (Tc1=3.6 K) and a lower nodeless gap state (Tc2=3.0 K), revealing degenerate superconducting states with different gap symmetries that split under strain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the nature of superconducting pairing symmetry in the kagome metal CsV₃Sb₅ and its relationship with the charge density wave (CDW) order, which are central unresolved issues in this material system.

Method: In-situ uniaxial pressure applied to CsV₃Sb₅ single crystals, combined with ¹²¹Sb nuclear quadrupole resonance (NQR) measurements, particularly the nuclear spin-lattice relaxation rate.

Result: Tensile strain significantly enhances Tc while leaving CDW order unchanged. At ε=+0.90%, a double superconducting transition is observed: Tc1=3.6 K (nodal gap) and Tc2=3.0 K (nodeless gap), indicating degenerate superconducting states with different gap symmetry that split under uniaxial strain.

Conclusion: Superconductivity can be tuned independently of bulk charge order in CsV₃Sb₅. The material hosts degenerate superconducting states with different gap symmetries at ambient pressure, which are split by uniaxial strain, demonstrating high tunability of superconductivity.

Abstract: The nature of the superconducting pairing symmetry in the kagome metal CsV$_3$Sb$_5$ and its relationship with the charge density wave (CDW) order are central unresolved issues. Here, we investigate the evolution of superconductivity in CsV$_3$Sb$_5$ under in-situ uniaxial pressure using $^{121}$Sb nuclear quadrupole resonance (NQR). We find that tensile strain significantly enhances the superconducting transition temperature, $T_{\rm c}$, while the CDW remains unchanged, demonstrating that superconductivity can be tuned independently of the bulk charge order. At a tensile strain of $\varepsilon$ = +0.90%, the nuclear spin-lattice relaxation rate reveals a remarkable double transition: an upper transition at $T_{\rm c1}$ = 3.6 K to a nodal gap state, and a lower one at $T_{\rm c2}$ = 3.0 K characterized by a nodeless gap. These results evidence degenerate superconducting states with different gap symmetry in the kagome metal at ambient pressure which split under strain. Our work demonstrates a high tunability of superconductivity by uniaxial pressure.

</details>


### [79] [Iron-Based Superconductors: A Decade of Materials, Magnetism, and Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11355)
*Xingye Lu,Hechang Lei,Jun Zhao,Hideo Hosono,Pengcheng Dai*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This review summarizes over a decade of progress in iron-based superconductors (FeSCs), focusing on experimental advances, pairing mechanisms, structural tuning, spin excitations, and material-specific gap symmetries, while highlighting emerging applications in thin-film and interface-engineered systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Iron-based superconductors provide a rich platform for studying high-temperature superconductivity in multiband, correlated electron systems. Understanding their phase diagrams, pairing mechanisms, and material diversity is crucial for both fundamental physics and technological applications.

Method: The paper reviews experimental advances including chemical substitution, pressure, epitaxial growth for structural tuning; inelastic neutron scattering for spin wave/excitation characterization; thin-film growth, intercalation chemistry, and interface engineering for enhanced Tc and device fabrication.

Result: High-energy spin excitations in doped FeSCs resemble parent spin waves; low-energy response reveals a superconducting resonance. Momentum structure of the resonance supports sign-changing pairing. Disorder, orbital fluctuations, and doping lead to material-dependent gap symmetries ($s_{\pm}$, $s_{++}$, nodal $s$, $d$-wave, multicomponent). FeSe-based systems show enhanced Tc via interface engineering.

Conclusion: FeSCs continue to advance both fundamental understanding of high-temperature superconductivity and practical applications due to high upper critical fields, moderate anisotropy, and improving current densities.

Abstract: Since its discovery in 2008, iron-based superconductors (FeSCs) have become a central platform for exploring high-temperature superconductivity in multiband, electron-correlated materials. This review focuses on major developments over the past decade or so, emphasizing experimental advances, pairing mechanisms, and emerging applications. Structural tuning through chemical substitution, pressure, and epitaxial growth enables precise control of the electronic, magnetic, and superconducting ground states, thereby revealing their interplay. In particular, the electronic nematic phase and stripe-type antiferromagnetic order-often coexisting or competing-are central to understanding the phase diagrams. Spin waves in magnetically ordered parent compounds and spin excitations (fluctuations) in doped superconductors are extensively characterized by inelastic neutron scattering. While high-energy spin excitations in doped superconductors retain substantial spectral weight across a wide energy range reminiscent of spin waves in their undoped parents, the low-energy response reveals a collective spin excitation termed ``resonance'' coupled to superconductivity. The momentum structure of superconductivity-induced resonance provides strong evidence for sign-changing pairing in many FeSCs, while disorder effects, orbital-fluctuation scenarios, quasiparticle damping, and compound-dependent gap structures indicate that $s_{\pm}$, $s_{++}$, nodal $s$, $d$-wave, and multicomponent states must be discussed in a material-specific framework. Advances in thin-film growth, intercalation chemistry, and interface engineering-particularly in FeSe-based systems-have enabled enhanced $T_{c}$ and novel device geometries. With high upper critical fields, moderate anisotropy, and improving current densities, FeSCs continue to drive both fundamental insight and technological applications in superconductivity.

</details>


### [80] [Interference-Enhanced Large Electron-Phonon Coupling from Raman-active Breathing Modes in Moiré Semiconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11425)
*Ning Mao,Shaozheng Wang,Cheng Xu,Xumin Chang,Kenji Watanabe,Takashi Taniguchi,Claudia Felser,Shengwei Jiang,Yang Zhang*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Combining Raman spectroscopy and machine-learning first-principles calculations, the authors reveal an interference selection rule governing electron-phonon coupling in twisted moiré semiconductors, identifying that only phonons with displacement textures matching the lattice-reconstruction pattern couple strongly, with peak coupling at large twist angles near where superconductivity appears, suggesting a substantial phonon contribution to pairing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether superconductivity in twisted WSe2 and MoTe2 is driven by electronic correlations, phonons, or both, which requires calculating electron-phonon coupling (EPC) in moiré semiconductors — a task beyond direct first-principles methods due to supercell sizes of thousands of atoms.

Method: Combining filling-dependent Raman spectroscopy with machine-learning first-principles calculations to obtain mode-by-mode EPC in supercells of up to tens of thousands of atoms.

Result: Discovery of an interference selection rule: a phonon couples strongly only when its displacement texture matches the static lattice-reconstruction pattern; otherwise coupling is suppressed. The coupling peaks at large twist angles, near where superconductivity appears.

Conclusion: Lattice-reconstruction interference serves as an organizing principle for moiré EPC, pointing to a substantial (potentially dominant) phonon contribution to pairing at large twist angles.

Abstract: Superconductivity was recently observed in twisted WSe2 and MoTe2, raising a central question: is the pairing driven by electronic correlations, by phonons, or by both? Answering it requires determining the electron-phonon coupling (EPC) in these moiré semiconductors, whose calculation in realistic supercells of thousands of atoms lies beyond the reach of direct first-principles methods. Here we combine filling-dependent Raman spectroscopy with machine-learning first-principles calculations to obtain the EPC mode by mode in supercells of up to tens of thousands of atoms. Raman reveals only a few moiré phonons whose frequencies shift strongly with filling; we trace this to an interference selection rule: a phonon couples strongly only when its displacement texture matches the static lattice-reconstruction pattern, and is otherwise suppressed by destructive interference. The rule selects the low- and high-frequency breathing modes seen in Raman and makes the coupling peak at large twist angles, near those at which superconductivity appears. Lattice-reconstruction interference thus emerges as an organizing principle for moiré EPC, pointing to a substantial, potentially dominant, phonon contribution to large-angle pairing.

</details>


### [81] [Microscopic equivalence of the vortex-entry current and the depairing current in a superconducting thin-film strip](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11518)
*Takayuki Kubo*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the vortex entry current in superconducting strips using Usadel theory, showing that the edge barrier disappearance current coincides with the depairing current determined by uniform perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To microscopically reformulate the identification of the vortex entry current in superconducting strips, moving beyond the Pearl-London phenomenological approach to a more fundamental understanding based on loss of local stability.

Method: Uses the fixed-current Gibbs functional of dirty-limit Usadel theory (valid at arbitrary temperatures) to analyze the stability of the vortex-free current-carrying state against both uniform and nonuniform perturbations in an ideal homogeneous thin-film strip.

Result: The barrier-disappearance current is set by the uniform perturbation and coincides exactly with the depairing current.

Conclusion: The vortex entry current in superconducting strips can be microscopically understood as the loss of local stability of the vortex-free state, with the critical current determined by the uniform perturbation mode, matching the depairing current.

Abstract: The current at which vortices enter a superconducting strip is usually identified in Pearl--London theory with the disappearance of an edge barrier. This work reformulates the same question microscopically as the loss of local stability of the vortex-free current-carrying state, using the fixed-current Gibbs functional of dirty-limit Usadel theory, which is valid at arbitrary temperatures. For an ideal homogeneous thin-film strip at zero applied field, with self-field effects neglected, the stability of the vortex-free state is examined against both spatially uniform and nonuniform perturbations. The barrier-disappearance current is shown to be set by the uniform perturbation and to coincide exactly with the depairing current.

</details>


### [82] [Correlation-renormalized spin-fluctuation pairing and the stabilization of $s_{\pm}$ superconductivity in pressurized La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11786)
*Shuhong Tang,Liang-Jian Zou*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: Using DMFT+RPA with self-energy renormalization, the authors find that strong correlations reverse the leading pairing symmetry in pressurized La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$ from $d_{xy}$ (bare RPA) to $s_{\pm}$ (renormalized), showing that correlation effects are essential for predicting superconductivity in this system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The superconducting gap symmetry of pressurized La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$ remains unsettled because conventional weak-coupling calculations place it near competing sign-changing $s$- and $d$-wave instabilities, necessitating a more accurate treatment of strong correlations.

Method: Combines single-site two-orbital dynamical mean-field theory (DMFT) with self-energy-renormalized random-phase approximation (RPA), replacing the bare particle-hole bubble $G_0G_0$ with $G_{\rm DMFT}G_{\rm DMFT}$, while keeping residual Slater-Kanamori interactions. Static spin susceptibility is validated via dual Bethe-Salpeter equation with local DMFT vertex.

Result: Bare RPA gives leading $B_{2g}$ $d_{xy}$ pairing; DMFT-renormalized RPA reverses hierarchy: $A_{1g}$ sign-changing $s_{\pm}$ becomes dominant, $B_{1g}$ $d_{x^2-y^2}$ is subleading, and $d_{xy}$ is strongly suppressed. The reversal originates from orbital-selective renormalization of $d_{3z^2-r^2}$ sector filtering $γ$-pocket scattering. Static susceptibility shows broad finite-momentum magnetic response favoring $s_{\pm}$.

Conclusion: Strong correlations are not a secondary correction in La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$; proper treatment of correlation-renormalized quasiparticles is essential for predicting the superconducting pairing symmetry.

Abstract: The superconducting gap symmetry of pressurized La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$ remains unsettled because conventional weak-coupling calculations often place the system close to competing sign-changing $s$- and $d$-wave instabilities. Using the four-orbital Wannier Hamiltonian of Xia et al., we combine single-site two-orbital dynamical mean-field theory (DMFT) with a self-energy-renormalized random-phase approximation (RPA). The central step is to replace the bare particle-hole bubble $G_0G_0$ of ordinary RPA by a $G_{\rm DMFT}G_{\rm DMFT}$ bubble, while keeping the same residual Slater--Kanamori interaction vertices. In the bare RPA benchmark, the leading pairing eigenvalue belongs to the $B_{2g}$ $d_{xy}$ channel. Once the DMFT self-energy is included, the hierarchy is reversed: the $A_{1g}$ sign-changing $s_{\pm}$ state becomes dominant, the $B_{1g}$ $d_{x^2-y^2}$ channel is subleading, and the original $B_{2g}$ instability is strongly suppressed. Pocket-pair decomposition and orbital-resolved susceptibilities show that the reversal originates from orbital-selective renormalization of the $d_{3z^2-r^2}$ sector, which filters the $γ$-pocket scattering processes that stabilize $d_{xy}$ pairing in bare RPA while preserving distributed inter-pocket processes favorable to $s_{\pm}$ pairing. As an independent two-particle validation, we further compute the static spin susceptibility using the dual Bethe--Salpeter equation with the local DMFT vertex. The resulting susceptibility retains a broad finite-momentum magnetic response and is weak near $Γ$, strengthening the spin-fluctuation background for the correlation-stabilized $s_{\pm}$ state. Our results demonstrate that strong correlations are not a secondary correction in La$_3$Ni$_2$O$_7$: an appropriate treatment of correlation-renormalized quasiparticles is essential for predicting the superconducting pairing symmetry.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.mes-hall'></div>

# cond-mat.mes-hall [[Back]](#toc)

### [83] [Staggered Potential and Elliptical Light Driven Topological Phase Transitions in $α$-$\mathcal{T}_{3}$ Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10153)
*Muhammad Faisal,Muhammad Irfan Sarwar*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: The paper investigates how hexagonal boron nitride (h-BN) substrate affects the electronic properties of an α-𝒯₃ lattice under elliptically polarized light, revealing a geometric singularity at α=1/√2 that creates a stable topological window for Chern insulator phases with C=1 and C=2, characterized by quantized anomalous Hall plateaus and distinct thermoelectric Seebeck responses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the combined influence of h-BN substrate (staggered potential) and off-resonant elliptically polarized light on the topological phase transitions in α-𝒯₃ lattice materials, and to identify experimentally accessible signatures of high-Chern-number phases.

Method: Theoretical analysis using a model of α-𝒯₃ lattice with staggered potential M from h-BN substrate, driven by off-resonant elliptically polarized light, studying band gap closings, Chern numbers, anomalous Hall conductivity, and thermoelectric Seebeck response.

Result: Discovery of a geometric singularity at α=1/√2 below which the lower band gap cannot close, yielding a stable topological window with C=1. Above this critical value, transitions from C=1 to C=2 occur. Quantized Hall plateaus at σ_xy=e²/h (C=1) and σ_xy=2e²/h (C=2) are found, with C=1 robust up to 300 K while C=2 requires T<100 K. Asymmetric thermoelectric response serves as experimental fingerprint.

Conclusion: The work provides a realistic framework for realizing stable high-Chern-number phases in substrate-supported α-𝒯₃ materials, with clear experimental signatures via Hall conductivity and thermoelectric measurements.

Abstract: We theoretically investigate the influence of hexagonal boron nitride (h-BN) on the electronic properties of an $α$-$\mathcal{T}_{3}$ lattice driven by an off-resonant elliptically polarized light field. The staggered potential $M$ breaks the sublattice inversion symmetry, transforming the initial semimetal into a trivial insulator with Chern number $C=0$. We identify a fundamental geometric singularity at $α=1/\sqrt{2}$, independent of $M$, below which no finite drive can close the lower band gap, creating a stable topological window where conduction--flat band inversion yields a Chern insulator with $C=1$. Above this critical value the lower gap closes with finite intensity, allowing a transition from $C=1$ to $C=2$ as the dice limit ($α=1$) is approached. The topological phases are characterized by quantized anomalous Hall plateaus at $σ_{xy}=e^2/h$ ($C=1$) and $σ_{xy}=2e^2/h$ ($C=2$), with each valley contributing exactly $\frac{1}{2}e^2/h$. The $C=1$ plateau remains robust from $0$ K to $300$ K, while $C=2$ requires $T<100$ K due to its narrower gap. A highly asymmetric thermoelectric Seebeck response further serves as an experimental fingerprint of each phase, providing a realistic framework for realizing stable high-Chern-number phases in substrate-supported $α$-$\mathcal{T}_{3}$ materials.

</details>


### [84] [Evidence for spin swapping from modulation of transverse resistance in magnetic heterostructures with Rashba interface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10189)
*Heeman Kim,Shutaro Karube,Juan Borge,Junyeon Kim,Kouta Kondou,YoshiChika Otani*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: The paper investigates transverse resistance in Cu/Bi₂O₃ and Ag/Bi₂O₃ Rashba interfaces under out-of-plane magnetic fields, finding opposite contributions that match the opposite signs of spin/charge interconversion, attributed to spin swapping of the converted spin current.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the transverse response and spin/charge interconversion mechanisms in magnetic heterostructures with Rashba interfaces, specifically the role of spin swapping in different metallic layers.

Method: Measurement of transverse resistance under out-of-plane magnetic fields in Cu/Bi₂O₃ and Ag/Bi₂O₃ heterostructures, comparing the responses with the signs of spin/charge interconversion from both interfaces.

Result: Opposite contributions to transverse resistance were observed for Cu/Bi₂O₃ versus Ag/Bi₂O₃ interfaces, which align with the opposite signs of spin/charge interconversion from the two interfaces.

Conclusion: The opposite influences on transverse resistance indicate spin swapping of the converted spin current; transverse spin flow occurs in both Cu and Ag layers but with opposite directions depending on the spin direction of the converted spin current.

Abstract: We investigate the transverse response under the out-of-plane magnetic field for magnetic heterostructures with Cu/Bi2O3 or Ag/Bi2O3 Rashba interfaces. We detect opposite contributions on the transverse resistance by the Cu/Bi2O3 and the Ag/Bi2O3 interfaces, which interestingly coincide well with the opposite signs of the spin/charge interconversion from the two interfaces. We suppose the opposite influences on the transverse resistance feature spin swapping occurrence of the converted spin current. The transverse spin flow emerges due to the spin swapping in both Cu and Ag layer, but the direction of the spin flow is opposed dependent on the spin direction of the converted spin current.

</details>


### [85] [Retarded interaction between opposite chiral edges in anomalous Hall crystals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10320)
*Wangqian Miao,Mu-Yang Chen,Binghai Yan,Chunli Huang*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: Anomalous Hall crystals in rhombohedral pentalayer graphene exhibit chiral edge modes and low-energy electronic phonons whose transverse bulk modes mediate retarded inter-edge coupling, producing a nonlocal dynamical response detectable via time-of-flight measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the dynamical consequences of coexisting spontaneous electronic crystallization and Chern insulating gaps, specifically how transverse bulk phonons in anomalous Hall crystals can mediate retarded interactions between counterpropagating chiral edge modes.

Method: Microscopic time-dependent Hartree-Fock calculations for rhombohedral pentalayer graphene, analyzing carrier density dependence of phonon softening and edge-phonon coupling.

Result: Lowering carrier density softens the long-wavelength transverse phonon mode; near instability, the boundary-projected phonon continuum overlaps with the edge dispersion, enabling coupling. A nonlocal measurement (drive on one edge, response on the other) provides a smoking-gun probe.

Conclusion: Anomalous Hall crystals realize a Luttinger-liquid variant with delayed inter-edge coupling via transverse phonons, distinct from ordinary Chern insulators.

Abstract: An anomalous Hall crystal combines spontaneous electronic crystallization with a Chern insulating gap, supporting both chiral edge modes and low-energy electronic phonons. We show that this coexistence produces a distinct dynamical effect from ordinary Chern insulators: transverse bulk phonons can mediate a retarded interaction between counterpropagating chiral edge modes on opposite sides of the sample, realizing a Luttinger-liquid variant with delayed inter-edge coupling. Using microscopic time-dependent Hartree--Fock calculations for rhombohedral pentalayer graphene, we find that lowering the carrier density softens the long-wavelength transverse phonon mode. Near this instability regime, the resulting boundary-projected phonon continuum inevitably overlaps with the edge dispersion, thereby enabling their coupling. A smoking-gun probe is a nonlocal measurement: a drive applied to one edge can induce a response on the other edge, delayed by the transverse phonon time of flight across the sample.

</details>


### [86] [Lifshitz-Kosevich Theory of Anomalous Landau Levels in Topological Flat Bands](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10328)
*Chao-Xing Liu*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: This paper extends Lifshitz-Kosevich theory to topological flat bands, showing that quantum oscillations can persist despite zero group velocity due to quantum geometry, and that thermal damping directly measures the quantum metric.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how anomalous Landau levels in topological flat bands behave within the Lifshitz-Kosevich framework and whether their thermal damping can reveal quantum geometric information.

Method: Using a minimal model with exactly flat topological bands, the authors derive an LK theory for anomalous Landau levels and analyze fixed-density magnetization oscillations.

Result: The oscillations exhibit a finite LK effective mass that is substantially larger than normal-band values and strongly field-dependent. In the weak-field limit, this anomalous mass scales inversely with both the magnetic field and the trace of the quantum metric.

Conclusion: Thermal damping of flat-band quantum oscillations directly measures the quantum metric, establishing quantum oscillations as a probe of flat-band quantum geometry.

Abstract: In conventional metals, quantum oscillations arise from Landau quantization of Fermi-surface cyclotron orbits, whose dynamics are governed by the Fermi velocity and cyclotron effective mass within Lifshitz-Kosevich (LK) theory. A perfectly flat band, by contrast, has vanishing group velocity, which would naively imply an infinite cyclotron mass and complete thermal suppression of quantum oscillations. Yet topological flat bands can support anomalous Landau levels (LLs) whose finite-field spacing is generated by quantum geometry rather than band curvature, allowing quantum oscillations to persist. This work addresses how such anomalous flat-band LLs behave within the LK framework and whether their thermal damping can reveal quantum geometric information. Using a minimal model with exactly flat topological bands, we derive an LK theory for these anomalous LLs and analyze fixed-density magnetization oscillations. The resulting oscillations exhibit a finite LK effective mass that is substantially larger than the normal-band value and possesses a strong magnetic-field dependence. In the weak-field limit, this anomalous mass reflects the quantum geometric origin of the LL spacing and scales inversely with both the magnetic field and the trace of the quantum metric. Thus, thermal damping of flat-band quantum oscillations directly measures the quantum metric, establishing quantum oscillations as a probe to flat-band quantum geometry.

</details>


### [87] [Staggered orbital magnetization from itinerant electrons: orbital antiferro- and ferrimagnetic phases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10355)
*Lucas L. Lage,Kevin J. U. Vidarte,Tarik P. Cysne,Tatiana G. Rappoport,A. Latgé,R. B. Muniz*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: This paper shows that itinerant electrons in solids can generate various orbital magnetic orders (ferromagnetic, antiferromagnetic, ferrimagnetic) beyond the usual net orbital magnetization, demonstrated in honeycomb lattices using Haldane models, with distinct phases arising from valley-dependent Dirac masses in the standard model and valley-dependent energy shifts in the modified model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate that electronic orbital angular momentum in solids, being inherently non-local, can generate orbital magnetic phases with ferromagnetic, antiferromagnetic, or ferrimagnetic orders, rather than being limited to a net orbital magnetization.

Method: The authors use a honeycomb lattice with both the standard and a modified Haldane model, employing real-space formulations to decompose itinerant orbital magnetization into sublattice contributions (M_A and M_B). They identify orbital order using net (M_z=M_A+M_B) and staggered (M_z^s=M_A-M_B) combinations, and explain findings via a low-energy theory with valley-dependent Dirac masses and energy shifts.

Result: Distinct regimes are identified: a PT-symmetric orbital antiferromagnet in the modified Haldane model, an orbital ferromagnet in the standard Haldane model, ferrimagnetic metallic states with coexisting net and staggered orbital magnetizations, and insulating regimes where ferro- and antiferromagnetic orbital characters can be interchanged.

Conclusion: Orbital magnetic phases beyond simple net magnetization exist in itinerant electron systems, controlled by sublattice potential and Fermi energy, with two distinct valley mechanisms (valley-dependent Dirac masses and valley-dependent energy shifts) governing the observed behavior.

Abstract: Because electronic orbital angular momentum in solids is inherently non-local, its contribution to magnetism is usually cast in terms of a net orbital magnetization. Here, we show that itinerant electrons can generate orbital magnetic phases with ferromagnetic, antiferromagnetic, or ferrimagnetic orders. We demonstrate this possibility in a honeycomb lattice, using both the standard and a modified Haldane model. Employing real-space formulations, we decompose the itinerant orbital magnetization into sublattice contributions, $M_A$ and $M_B$. Their net ($M_z=M_A+M_B$) and staggered ($M_z^s=M_A-M_B$) combinations are then used to identify the orbital order. By varying the sublattice potential and the Fermi energy, we find distinct regimes: a (PT)-symmetric orbital antiferromagnet in the modified Haldane model, an orbital ferromagnet in the standard Haldane model, ferrimagnetic metallic states where net and staggered orbital magnetizations coexist, and insulating regimes in which the ferro- and antiferromagnetic orbital characters can be interchanged.These findings are explained by a low-energy theory in terms of two distinct valley mechanisms: valley-dependent Dirac masses in the standard Haldane model and valley-dependent energy shifts in its modified version.

</details>


### [88] [Nonperturbative magnetotransport from band geometry in Weyl semimetals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10468)
*L. Medel Onofre,A. Martín-Ruiz*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: This paper develops a nonperturbative semiclassical theory of magnetotransport in Weyl semimetals, showing that the magnetic-field expansion and momentum integration do not commute, leading to nonanalytic contributions that make magnetotransport intrinsically nonperturbative at low carrier densities or moderate magnetic fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a nonperturbative semiclassical theory that retains the full magnetic-field dependence of the Fermi-surface conductivity in Weyl semimetals, addressing the intrinsic infrared sensitivity associated with the singular behavior of the orbital magnetic moment.

Method: A nonperturbative semiclassical framework incorporating Berry curvature and orbital magnetic moment effects, with closed-form expressions valid to all orders in the magnetic field, combined with a physically motivated regularization to handle singular orbital magnetic moment behavior.

Result: The magnetic-field expansion and momentum integration do not commute, producing nonanalytic contributions at the level of scalar transport coefficients. A regime exists at low carrier densities or moderate magnetic fields where magnetotransport is intrinsically nonperturbative and beyond conventional weak-field expansions.

Conclusion: Standard quadratic magnetoconductivity expansions fail in certain physically relevant regimes, and a nonperturbative treatment is required to correctly capture magnetotransport in Weyl semimetals.

Abstract: We develop a nonperturbative semiclassical theory of magnetotransport in Weyl semimetals, retaining the full magnetic-field dependence of the Fermi-surface conductivity in the presence of Berry curvature and orbital magnetic moment effects. We obtain closed-form expressions valid to all orders in the magnetic field within the semiclassical regime. We show that the exact continuum formulation exhibits an intrinsic infrared sensitivity associated with the singular behavior of the orbital magnetic moment, requiring a physically motivated regularization. While the full conductivity tensor reduces to the standard quadratic magnetoconductivity, we demonstrate that magnetic-field expansion and momentum integration do not commute, leading to nonanalytic contributions at the level of scalar transport coefficients. Our results identify a regime, relevant for low carrier densities or moderate magnetic fields, where magnetotransport becomes intrinsically nonperturbative and cannot be captured by conventional weak-field expansions.

</details>


### [89] [Spectral-Domain Deep Learning of Intrinsic Scattering Operators for Arbitrarily Shaped Compact 3D Particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10570)
*Daize Li,Jiafu Shen,Yifei Liu,Bonan Zhang,Heping Xie*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: A dual spectral-domain neural scattering model (spectral-token Transformer) maps particle geometry (compressed to 256 spherical-harmonic coefficients) directly to the T-matrix (spherical-vector-wave basis), enabling rapid, reusable, angle- and polarization-resolved optical scattering prediction for arbitrarily shaped 3D particles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rapid prediction of optical scattering from arbitrarily shaped 3D particles is important but challenging due to large variability of complex morphologies and strong angular dependence of scattering responses.

Method: A dual spectral-domain neural scattering model: (1) particle geometry compressed into 256 spherical-harmonic coefficients (morphology spectrum); (2) optical response encoded by complex T-matrix in spherical-vector-wave basis; (3) a spectral-token Transformer trained on 50,000 irregular particles at 1064 nm maps morphology spectrum to T-matrix.

Result: The predicted operators recover modal structure, reproduce full-angle differential scattering maps and incidence-angle scans. Generalizes well to out-of-distribution synthetic shapes and natural sand-particle morphologies.

Conclusion: Spectral-domain operator learning establishes a compact route for reusable, angle- and polarization-resolved optical scattering prediction of complex 3D particles.

Abstract: Rapid prediction of optical scattering from arbitrarily shaped three-dimensional particles is important for particle optics and photonic characterization, but remains challenging because of the large variability of complex morphologies and the strong angular dependence of their scattering responses. To address both issues, a dual spectral-domain neural scattering model is introduced in which morphology and scattering are represented in physically ordered bases: particle geometry is compressed into only 256 spherical-harmonic coefficients, and the optical response is encoded by the complex T-matrix in a spherical-vector-wave basis. The morphology spectrum replaces high-dimensional Euclidean geometry representations, such as voxel grids, point clouds, or meshes, with a compact ordered descriptor, while the T-matrix represents a geometry-determined scattering operator that can be queried for different incidence directions, polarizations, and observation angles. A spectral-token Transformer trained on 50{,}000 irregular particles at 1064~nm maps the morphology spectrum directly to the T-matrix. The predicted operators recover modal structure and reproduce full-angle differential scattering maps and incidence-angle scans. Generalization to out-of-distribution synthetic shapes and natural sand-particle morphologies shows that the dual spectral architecture learns an intrinsic relation from the geometry spectrum to multipolar scattering. This establishes spectral-domain operator learning as a compact route for reusable, angle- and polarization-resolved optical scattering prediction of complex 3D particles.

</details>


### [90] [Transport in magnetic-topological-insulator nanoribbons containing multiple superconductor-proximitized sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10665)
*Javier Osca,Sungguen Ryu,Rosa López,Llorenç Serra*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: The paper studies how complex pairing phases control chiral Majorana transport in magnetic topological insulator nanoribbons with multiple proximitized sectors, predicting AC Majorana effects and phase-configurable Majorana routers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Transport in devices with multiple proximitized sectors depends heavily on the complex phases of the pairing gaps, yet this dependence is not well understood for chiral Majorana modes.

Method: The authors theoretically investigate magnetic topological insulator nanoribbons in two- and three-terminal setups with proximitized sectors and asymptotic normal leads, focusing on the single chiral Majorana regime.

Result: The complex phases of pairing control electric and thermal interferometries of chiral Majoranas. An AC Majorana effect is predicted where voltage-induced phase dynamics produce measurable time-dependent conductance oscillations. A three-terminal junction with superconducting islands can function as a Majorana router via relative phase configuration.

Conclusion: Complex pairing phases provide a controllable mechanism for Majorana interferometry and routing, enabling new phase-controlled Majorana devices.

Abstract: Transport in devices with multiple proximitized sectors depends heavily on the complex phases of the pairing gaps of those sectors. We investigate magnetic topological insulator nanoribbons in two- and three-terminal setups with proximitized sectors and asymptotic normal leads. Our focus is on the regime of single chiral Majoranas. The characteristic electric and thermal interferometries of chiral Majoranas can be controlled by the complex phases of the pairing. We predict an AC Majorana effect, in which phase dynamics induced by a voltage bias generate measurable time-dependent conductance oscillations. A three-terminal junction with superconducting islands can be used as a Majorana router when the relative complex phases are configured.

</details>


### [91] [Parity-driven RKKY decoupling and anomalous $1/R$ Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction in $p$-wave magnets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10757)
*Morteza Salehi,Tohid Farajollahpour*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: The paper investigates Ruderman-Kittel-Kasuya-Yosida (RKKY) indirect exchange in two-dimensional p-wave magnets with Rashba spin-orbit coupling, revealing parity-driven spatial decoupling, tunable Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interactions, and an anomalous dimension-reducing crossover in chiral decay behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the magnetic exchange interactions in unconventional p-wave magnets, which offer a distinct odd-parity momentum-dependent spin splitting paradigm for non-collinear spintronics, and to explore how the interplay between p-wave nodal geometry and Rashba coupling leads to novel magnetic behaviors.

Method: Analytical real-space Green's function formalism is used to theoretically study RKKY exchange in a 2D p-wave magnet with Rashba spin-orbit coupling.

Result: (1) Out-of-plane Ising interaction is structurally insulated from p-wave modulation, oscillating isotropically at the shifted Fermi wavevector. (2) In-plane Heisenberg components exhibit directionally tunable spatial beating. (3) Hybridized bands generate a tunable three-component Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction and symmetric off-diagonal anisotropies. (4) Out-of-plane chiral twisting is driven by massive nonrelativistic p-wave momentum shift, while in-plane chiral components are strictly relativistic. (5) Competition between p-wave nodal geometry and Rashba gap drives an anomalous dimension-reducing crossover: in-plane chiral components follow 1D-like 1/R decay along nodal lines before recovering 2D 1/R² asymptote.

Conclusion: p-wave magnets are promising platforms for engineering robust, directionally tunable non-collinear spin textures, with potential applications in spintronics.

Abstract: Unconventional $p$-wave magnets, characterized by an odd-parity momentum-dependent spin splitting, offer a fundamentally distinct paradigm for non-collinear spintronics. Here, we theoretically investigate the Ruderman-Kittel-Kasuya-Yosida indirect exchange in a two-dimensional $p$-wave magnet subjected to Rashba spin-orbit coupling. Using an analytical real-space Green's function formalism, we uncover a parity-driven spatial decoupling in the magnetic response. Because of the odd-parity exchange field, the out-of-plane Ising interaction is structurally insulated from the macroscopic $p$-wave modulation, oscillating isotropically at the shifted Fermi wavevector. Conversely, the in-plane Heisenberg components exhibit pronounced, directionally tunable spatial beating. Beyond collinear exchange, the hybridized bands generate a highly tunable, three-component Dzyaloshinskii-Moriya interaction alongside symmetric off-diagonal anisotropies. We reveal that the out-of-plane chiral twisting is driven by the massive, nonrelativistic $p$-wave momentum shift, while the in-plane chiral components are strictly relativistic. Furthermore, the competition between the $p$-wave nodal geometry and the Rashba gap drives an anomalous, dimension-reducing crossover, in which the in-plane chiral components follow a 1D-like $1/R$ spatial decay along the nodal lines over an extended intermediate-distance window before ultimately recovering the conventional 2D $1/R^2$ asymptote. These findings establish $p$-wave magnets as promising platforms for engineering robust, directionally tunable non-collinear spin textures.

</details>


### [92] [Single-Contact Problem in Atomically Flat Interfaces: a Simulation Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10979)
*Rui Dong,Ahmed Uluca,Graham Cross,Stefano Sanvito*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: This paper combines atomistic simulations and a modified continuum model to investigate friction at crystalline SiO₂/SiO₂ interfaces, finding that shear strength and minimal-scale sliding friction depend linearly on normal pressure below 3 GPa with non-zero values at zero pressure. The authors extend the Mindlin model with adhesion and nanoscale effects to describe stick-slip transitions under Hertzian stress, showing nonuniform pressure distributions lower effective static friction. Experiments on graphene-passivated contacts validate the model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between nanoscale structural superlubricity and realistic tribological systems by understanding friction at single-asperity contacts under Hertzian contact geometry.

Method: Atomistic simulations combined with a modified continuum model; computation of interfacial sliding potential energy surfaces (ISPES) to determine load-dependent shear strength and minimal-scale sliding (MSS) friction; extension of the classical Mindlin model by incorporating adhesion and nanoscale load effects.

Result: Shear strength and MSS friction depend linearly on normal pressure below 3 GPa with non-zero values at zero pressure. Nonuniform pressure distributions substantially lower effective static friction. Oscillatory-shear experiments on graphene-passivated contacts reproduce the predicted stiffness-collapse signature and adhesion-limited shear strength.

Conclusion: The modified Mindlin model incorporating adhesion and nanoscale effects accurately describes stick-slip transitions under realistic Hertzian stress distributions, with experimental validation supporting its relevance to real micro-asperity tribology.

Abstract: Understanding friction at single-asperity contacts is essential for bridging the gap between nanoscale structural superlubricity and realistic tribological systems dominated by Hertzian contact geometry. In this work, we combine atomistic simulations and a modified continuum model to investigate the onset of sliding at crystalline SiO$_2$/SiO$_2$ interfaces. Interfacial sliding potential energy surfaces (ISPES) are computed to determine the load-dependent shear strength and minimal-scale sliding (MSS) friction. Both quantities exhibit linear dependence on normal pressure below 3 GPa, and have non-zero values at zero pressure. Incorporating these parameters, we extend the classical Mindlin model by including adhesion and nanoscale load effects, allowing us to describe the stick to slip transition under realistic Hertzian stress distributions. The model shows that nonuniform pressure distributions substantially lower the effective static friction, and oscillatory-shear experiments on graphene-passivated contacts reproduce both the predicted stiffness-collapse signature and, in the passivated limit, the adhesion-limited shear strength obtained from simulation, supporting the model's relevance to real micro-asperity tribology.

</details>


### [93] [Layer-Resolved Topological Metals in the Bilayer Lieb Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11009)
*Mengjie Yang,S Rahul,Giandomenico Palumbo*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: The paper identifies a topological metallic phase on a bilayer Lieb lattice with quantized layer-resolved pseudo-spin Chern number, and demonstrates how intralayer OAM-dependent coupling transforms a topological semimetal into a metal with asymmetric edge states, including flat bands and one-dimensional Dirac cones, enabling controlled engineering of layer-resolved topological gapless phases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore and characterize a two-dimensional time-reversal-invariant topological metallic phase with quantized layer-resolved pseudo-spin Chern numbers, and to understand how orbital-angular-momentum-dependent (OAM-dependent) coupling modifies the topological and boundary properties.

Method: The study uses a bilayer Lieb lattice model and analyzes the effects of intralayer OAM-dependent coupling on topological invariants (pseudo-spin Chern numbers), spectral gaps, and edge state behavior through theoretical and possibly tight-binding calculations.

Result: A time-reversal-invariant topological semimetal with zero indirect gap and quantized pseudo-spin Chern number is found. Intralayer OAM coupling converts it into a metal while preserving the Chern number. Asymmetric edge states emerge: flat bands on one edge and a 1D Dirac cone on the opposite edge. Edge-localized interlayer coupling gaps only the Dirac edge, while OAM coupling bends the flat band.

Conclusion: The findings provide a pathway for controlled engineering of layer-resolved topological gapless phases in synthetic and quantum materials.

Abstract: We identify a two-dimensional time-reversal-invariant topological metallic phase on a bilayer Lieb lattice, characterized by a quantized layer--resolved pseudo-spin Chern number. Without the orbital-angular-momentum-dependent (OAM-dependent) coupling, the system gives rise to a time-reversal-invariant topological semimetal with a zero indirect gap and quantized pseudo-spin Chern number.
